\section{Tracked-File Audit Round}

On 2026-05-21, the audit ran a documentation-only tracked-file review before
making additional logic changes.  The scope was the current
\texttt{lean-fast-path} worktree: \texttt{git ls-files | wc -l} reported 134
tracked files, source-like files totalled 43,009 lines, and
\texttt{pytest --collect-only -q} collected 823 tests.  The local environment
did not have the \texttt{coverage} package installed, so this round used
subagent source review, static symbol scans, direct test-reference checks, and
tracked text searches instead of line coverage.

Five read-only subagents inspected disjoint file groups covering the core
runtime, kernel/C++ runtime, public API/workflow modules, Rust/scripts/configs
and examples, and tests/docs/package metadata.  The consolidated record is now
\texttt{docs/repo-wide-audit-2026-05-21.md}.  It documents current gaps before
logic changes, including small-species backward semantics, optional
\texttt{ancestors\_T} parameters that are required in practice, duplicate direct
\texttt{family\_names}, soft \texttt{clade\_budget} behavior, adaptive trace
semantics, DTS parameter-shape ambiguity, unvalidated C++ \texttt{max\_wave\_size}
exports, thin backward-kernel coverage, stale CUDA tuning flags, possible
unused pybind debug exports, \texttt{BatchedLBFGS.max\_eval}, solver
reconfiguration during lazy prefetch, missing sampling subprocess timeouts,
sampling aggregate format ambiguity, fixed-path legacy HOGENOM launchers,
stale benchmark/report references, Rust backtracking numeric schema gaps,
release license blockers, brittle release text mirrors, broad warning
suppression, stale test package docs, and release-test sensitivity to a stale
installed \texttt{gpurec} console script on \texttt{PATH}.

The docs map now treats \texttt{core-simplification-suggestions.md} as a
historical snapshot rather than a current operating note because it contains
already-completed cleanup entries.  The new audit note gives the next change
order: first fix documentation-only staleness, then add contract tests that pin
current behavior, then make low-risk hygiene edits, and only after that change
runtime semantics.

The \texttt{BatchedLBFGS.max\_eval} follow-up is specifically constrained to
respect the configured evaluation budget in both the persisted
\texttt{func\_evals} counter and the actual closure invocations.  If the line
search consumes the final allowed evaluation, the optimizer must not fabricate a
curvature pair for the accepted step; history updates require a fresh gradient
computed inside the budget.

Verification during this round collected 823 tests.  The release metadata
checker still failed only on the known license blockers.  The repository
hygiene suite passed, and \texttt{python -m gpurec.cli --help} passed.  A
combined release-metadata/hygiene run failed one CLI help smoke because
\texttt{gpurec} on \texttt{PATH} resolved to an older installed script importing
\texttt{gpurec.cli.reconcile}; the repo-local module path did not show that
failure.

A follow-up documentation-only cleanup then removed the tracked reference to
the missing forward/backward pipeline plan, marked historical lean-regression
benchmark references as provenance rather than a current command set, replaced
the missing global-parameter benchmark script in the second-order optimization
note with future-harness wording, documented fixed-dataset HOGENOM launcher
paths in \texttt{scripts/README.md}, and annotated the Hydra HOGENOM YAML as a
checkout-local full experiment config.  A tracked grep for the stale reference
tokens found no remaining matches afterward.  The benchmark harness still
compiled, and the repository hygiene plus examples suites passed
\texttt{23 passed}.

The README now documents that \texttt{examples/minimal-run-config.json} is a
CUDA smoke for the retained optimized path, defines the sampling aggregate text
formats and sample-count normalization, and labels the Hydra HOGENOM YAML as a
checkout-local full experiment config.  The tests README now explicitly states
that some CUDA-contract tests still live under \texttt{tests/unit} and should be
deselected with \texttt{-m "unit and not gpu"} for CPU-only gates.

The release metadata CLI smoke now skips only the known stale external
\texttt{gpurec} script that imports \texttt{gpurec.cli.reconcile}, while still
running the repo-local \texttt{python -m gpurec.cli --help} smoke.  The focused
release metadata suite reported \texttt{39 passed, 1 skipped}; the hygiene plus
examples suite reported \texttt{23 passed}.

A contract regression now pins the documented \texttt{clade\_budget} behavior:
both first-fit planners treat the budget as a packing target, so an individual
oversized family is allowed in its own batch while smaller packable families
remain under the target.  The focused test reported \texttt{2 passed}, and the
full global wave scheduler suite reported \texttt{40 passed}.

The retained uniform-transfer E solver now documents and validates that
\texttt{ancestors\_T} is required.  This turns the previously indirect
matrix-multiply failure into a clear \texttt{ValueError}.  Focused coverage for
\texttt{E\_step} and \texttt{E\_fixed\_point} missing-\texttt{ancestors\_T}
reported \texttt{6 passed} in \texttt{tests/unit/test\_origination\_probs.py}.
The CPU unit gate after these contract cleanups reported
\texttt{798 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

\texttt{GeneDataset} now rejects duplicate direct \texttt{family\_names} before
loading the preprocessing extension, matching the AleRax family parser's
no-duplicate-name contract.  The focused validation test reported
\texttt{1 passed}; the paired pre-extension validation slice reported
\texttt{2 passed}.  The CPU unit gate after this direct-constructor guard
reported \texttt{799 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

\texttt{BatchedLBFGS} now respects tight \texttt{max\_eval} budgets across both
the persisted \texttt{func\_evals} counter and actual closure invocations.  The
regression first failed with \texttt{func\_evals == 3} for
\texttt{max\_eval=2}; after the fix, the post-line-search gradient refresh is
performed only when budget remains, and curvature history is skipped when no
fresh gradient pair exists.  The focused regression passed, and the full
\texttt{tests/unit/test\_batched\_lbfgs.py} module reported \texttt{6 passed}.

The shared backtracking sampler subprocess helper now uses a finite one-hour
timeout.  \texttt{subprocess.TimeoutExpired} is converted to the same
no-traceback \texttt{RuntimeError} style already used for nonzero sampler
exits, including the command text and timeout value.  The regression first
failed because \texttt{TimeoutExpired} propagated directly and the sampler call
had no \texttt{timeout}; after the fix, the focused timeout test passed, the
neighboring subprocess-runner slice reported \texttt{4 passed}, and the full
\texttt{tests/unit/test\_workflow.py} module reported \texttt{423 passed}.

The direct C++ \texttt{max\_wave\_size} follow-up is now complete for the
pybind-exposed scheduler diagnostics.  Each exported scheduler that accepts a
wave cap rejects \texttt{max\_wave\_size <= 0} before parsing input files, and
the repository hygiene guard verifies that the exported entry points share the
same validator.  The guard first failed because the shared validator did not
exist; after the fix, the focused hygiene test passed, the full repository
hygiene suite reported \texttt{20 passed}, the C++ extension build/load probe
printed \texttt{preprocess\_cpp}, and direct pybind probes over all six exported
wave-cap diagnostics raised \texttt{ValueError: max\_wave\_size must be
positive}.

The current full collection reports \texttt{831 tests collected}, and the
CPU-only unit gate reports \texttt{802 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The include and test-package hygiene cleanup is now complete.  The new
repository hygiene guards first failed because \texttt{preprocess.cpp} lacked a
direct \texttt{<chrono>} include and \texttt{tests/\_\_init\_\_.py} still
mentioned stale \texttt{gradients/} and \texttt{performance/} directories.
After the cleanup, \texttt{preprocess.cpp} includes \texttt{<chrono>} and
\texttt{<set>} directly, and \texttt{tests/\_\_init\_\_.py} documents the
current \texttt{unit/}, \texttt{integration/}, and \texttt{kernels/} layout plus
the retained \texttt{tests.unit.alerax\_helpers} import namespace.  The focused
hygiene tests passed, the full repository hygiene suite reported
\texttt{22 passed}, and the C++ extension build/load probe printed
\texttt{preprocess\_cpp}.

The later C++ export audit found that the only remaining
\texttt{std::chrono} use was the unowned \texttt{bench\_parse} pybind benchmark
lambda.  That helper is not used by supported Python workflows or tests, while
the retained wave-stat exports have explicit \texttt{max\_wave\_size}
validation guards.  \texttt{bench\_parse} and the now-unneeded
\texttt{<chrono>} include are removed, keeping \texttt{<set>} as the direct
standard include required by retained source.  A C++ extension build/load probe
printed \texttt{preprocess\_cpp}, confirmed all six retained wave-stat
diagnostic exports are present, and confirmed \texttt{has\_bench\_parse=False}.
The focused repository hygiene selector reports \texttt{2 passed}, the full
repository hygiene module reports \texttt{36 passed}, full collection reports
\texttt{859 tests collected}, and the CPU-only unit gate reports
\texttt{830 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The \texttt{Pi\_wave\_backward} signature cleanup is now documented before the
logic change.  Source inspection found two production call sites, in
\texttt{gpurec/optimization/implicit\_grad.py} and
\texttt{gpurec/api/uniform\_chunked.py}.  Both pass \texttt{ancestors\_T} to
the Pi-adjoint function even though that function never reads it; the
E-adjoint and likelihood paths keep separate \texttt{ancestors\_T} parameters
where the sparse ancestor matrix is still required.  Removing the argument only
from \texttt{Pi\_wave\_backward} therefore narrows the Pi-adjoint contract
without changing E-solver behavior.  The unused keyword is now removed from
\texttt{Pi\_wave\_backward} and its two production call sites, and a repository
hygiene guard checks that the signature and call sites stay clean.  The touched
Python modules pass \texttt{py\_compile}, the focused signature guard reports
\texttt{1 passed}, and the adjacent backward/implicit-gradient/hygiene slice
reports \texttt{40 passed}.  Full collection reports \texttt{860 tests
collected}, and the CPU-only unit gate reports
\texttt{831 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The current full collection reports \texttt{833 tests collected}, and the
CPU-only unit gate reports \texttt{804 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The warning-filter cleanup is constrained to pytest configuration.  A CPU-only
unit run with \texttt{-W default} did not surface known warning noise, so the
blanket \texttt{DeprecationWarning} and \texttt{PendingDeprecationWarning}
ignores can be removed rather than replaced with scoped filters.  A repository
hygiene guard should reject future broad warning ignores while still allowing
targeted filters if a dependency later needs one.

The warning-filter cleanup is now complete.  \texttt{pytest.ini} no longer
globally ignores all deprecation warnings, and the repository hygiene suite now
guards against reintroducing blanket deprecation-warning ignores.  The guard
first failed on the existing \texttt{ignore::PendingDeprecationWarning} entry,
then passed after the filter block was removed.  The full repository hygiene
suite reported \texttt{23 passed}, and the CPU-only unit gate under the actual
configuration reported \texttt{805 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The current full collection reports \texttt{834 tests collected}.

The marker-classification cleanup is now complete.  CUDA unit tests that depend
on the 1000-family fixture remain under \texttt{tests/unit/} for ownership, but
now carry local \texttt{@pytest.mark.slow} decorators.  A repository hygiene
guard detects direct and fixture-mediated \texttt{data\_dir\_1000} usage in unit
tests and requires those tests to be locally marked as slow.  The slow
collection now reports \texttt{6/835 tests collected}, \texttt{gpu and not slow}
reports \texttt{21/835 tests collected}, the full repository hygiene suite
reports \texttt{24 passed}, and the CPU-only unit gate reports
\texttt{806 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The current full collection reports \texttt{835 tests collected}.

The core refresh explorer reported no edits and confirmed several deletion
candidates already visible in the audit backlog: pybind-only scheduler
diagnostics, the legacy \texttt{preprocess()} wrapper, the
\texttt{include\_details=False} C++ extension path, and scheduler helpers that
now appear test-only.  It also surfaced a user-facing documentation gap for the
\texttt{GPUREC\_*} runtime environment flags.  The README now documents those
flags by scope, including binary discovery, compatibility guards, memory
policy, retained CUDA/Triton fast-path selectors, and diagnostic launch tuning.
A repository hygiene guard scans tracked package code for \texttt{GPUREC\_*}
variables and requires each to appear in the README.  The focused guard passed,
the full repository hygiene suite reports \texttt{25 passed}, the full
collection reports \texttt{836 tests collected}, and the CPU-only unit gate
reports \texttt{807 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The next profiling-script cleanup is documentation-only.  The
\texttt{profiling/evaluate\_hogenom\_alerax\_rates.py} helper is a
checkout-local HOGENOM AleRax validation utility: it reads one AleRax checkpoint
rate file per family, treats the second line's first three columns as D/L/T,
and defaults to local HOGENOM paths plus CUDA.  Before any parser or workflow
logic changes, the script and help text should make those assumptions explicit
and a source guard should keep the warning visible.

That profiling-script cleanup is now complete.  The script module docstring and
argparse help now state that it is a checkout-local HOGENOM AleRax validation
utility, not a general rate-file parser, and they document the per-family
checkpoint file layout plus the second-line D/L/T rate assumption.  The focused
source guard passed, \texttt{python profiling/evaluate\_hogenom\_alerax\_rates.py
--help} passed, the repository hygiene suite reports \texttt{26 passed}, the
full collection reports \texttt{837 tests collected}, and the CPU-only unit
gate reports \texttt{808 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The refreshed API/workflow audit found one small public branch still missing a
direct guard: \texttt{UniformChunkedReconModel.loss\_and\_grad(reduction=
"full\_sum\_estimate")} scales the selected chunk loss and gradient by
\texttt{total\_families / selected\_families}.  Existing GPU integration tests
cover the default, \texttt{sum}, and \texttt{mean} reductions, but not this
scaling branch.  A CPU-safe unit test can cover the contract by monkeypatching
the internal chunk evaluator instead of constructing a CUDA model.

The \texttt{full\_sum\_estimate} guard is now in place.  It monkeypatches the
chunk evaluator, calls \texttt{loss\_and\_grad()} on a lightweight
\texttt{UniformChunkedReconModel} instance, and asserts that both the loss and
gradient are multiplied by \texttt{total\_families / selected\_families}.  The
focused test first exposed a test-harness issue from bypassing
\texttt{torch.nn.Module.\_\_init\_\_()}, then passed after initializing the
module before assigning \texttt{theta}.  The full
\texttt{tests/unit/test\_optimization\_workflow.py} module reports
\texttt{29 passed}, the full collection reports \texttt{838 tests collected},
and the CPU-only unit gate reports
\texttt{809 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The refreshed API/workflow audit also identified
\texttt{gpurec.workflow.checkpoint.load\_checkpoint\_config()} as unsupported
surface: it is not exported by \texttt{gpurec.workflow}, not exported at the
package top level, and search finds only the definition.  The cleanup is to
remove that helper while keeping the documented checkpoint functions
\texttt{save\_checkpoint}, \texttt{load\_checkpoint}, and
\texttt{restore\_model\_theta}.

That helper has been removed.  Search now finds
\texttt{load\_checkpoint\_config} only in audit documentation, and a direct
import probe confirms that
\texttt{hasattr(gpurec.workflow.checkpoint, "load\_checkpoint\_config")}
returns \texttt{False}.  The checkpoint-focused workflow slice reports
\texttt{50 passed, 373 deselected}, the full
\texttt{tests/unit/test\_workflow.py} module reports \texttt{423 passed}, the
full collection remains \texttt{838 tests collected}, and the CPU-only unit
gate remains \texttt{809 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The next public API coverage gap is
\texttt{GeneReconModel.materialize\_batches()} and
\texttt{GeneReconModel.full\_loss\_for\_theta(theta)}.  These helpers are part
of the documented resident-batch and explicit-theta optimization surface, but
their exact contracts are only indirectly exercised by CUDA integration tests.
Before touching runtime behavior, add CPU-safe guards that
\texttt{materialize\_batches()} builds every resident batch and returns a copy of
batch metadata, and that \texttt{full\_loss\_for\_theta(theta)} selects the
\texttt{need\_grad=True} streaming path for differentiable explicit-theta probes
and the \texttt{need\_grad=False} path for no-grad probes.

Those public API guards are now in place in
\texttt{tests/unit/test\_workflow.py}.  The focused selector reports
\texttt{3 passed, 423 deselected}, the full workflow module reports
\texttt{426 passed}, and a direct \texttt{py\_compile} pass succeeds for the
edited test module.

The Rust crate audit read all tracked files under
\texttt{crates/gpurec-backtrack}.  It found that
\texttt{Matrix::validate()} computes \texttt{rows * cols} directly, so extreme
payload dimensions can panic in debug builds before the sampler returns
\texttt{InvalidInput}.  It also found missing public schema documentation for
row-major matrices, base-2 log units, the \texttt{-1e300} sentinel, postorder
species indexing, leaf/split bounds, and origination probability semantics.
Before sampler behavior changes, document that schema and add a regression that
matrix shape overflow is rejected normally.  Remaining Rust follow-ups are to
tighten Rust-side numeric validation to match the Python bridge and test more
sampler branches.  Later audit passes closed the single-sample
\texttt{--output-dir} plus output-file parser gap, removed the direct
\texttt{quick-xml} dev-dependency, and replaced
\texttt{WorkItem.clade: Option<usize>} with a plain \texttt{usize}.

The workflow/CLI/optimizer audit read all tracked files in its scope and ran a
focused unit slice reporting \texttt{557 passed}.  Its highest-priority
documentation gaps are optimizer modes and resume semantics: the CLI exposes
\texttt{adagrad}, \texttt{lbfgs}, and \texttt{adam-lbfgs}, but README-level docs
still mainly describe Adam, and completed-checkpoint resume status is not
specified.  It also flagged adjoint solver failure diagnostics, the apparently
test-only \texttt{safe\_float()} helper, and the lack of a maintained
\texttt{RunConfig}/CLI option reference.  These should be documented before any
optimizer, resume, or validation behavior changes.

The tests audit read every tracked file under \texttt{tests/**} plus
\texttt{pytest.ini}.  It found one actionable overbroad skip in
\texttt{tests/integration/test\_uniform\_chunked\_model.py}: the whole module
requires \texttt{test\_trees\_1000}, even though the HOGENOM unrooted-Newick
test only needs \texttt{hogenom\_bench}.  It also flagged smoke-heavy GPU
assertions, missing loose-tolerance adaptive-iteration coverage, intentionally
private guardrail tests that need a policy in \texttt{tests/README.md}, brittle
wording snapshots, and duplicated import/export coverage.

The docs/scripts audit read the docs, scripts, profiling, examples, configs,
notebooks, README, manifest, and pyproject surfaces.  It confirmed the known
license metadata blocker and added that the tracked notebooks need explicit
checkout-local HOGENOM ownership docs, the large HOGENOM launchers and stale
report scripts need a keep/delete/migrate matrix, the retained benchmark should
be documented as an internal private-API benchmark, historical docs need a
stale-reference convention, and the W\&B config needs a smoke/offline override
snippet before it is treated as a reusable example.

The Rust matrix overflow fix is now implemented after that documentation pass:
public schema comments cover row-major matrices, base-2 log values, the
\texttt{-1e300} sentinel, postorder species indexing, and origination weight
semantics; \texttt{Matrix::validate()} now uses checked multiplication; the
focused overflow regression passes; and
\texttt{cargo test --locked --manifest-path crates/gpurec-backtrack/Cargo.toml}
reports \texttt{10} library tests and \texttt{4} CLI tests passing.

The core/API audit read every tracked file in its slice.  Its remaining
highest-risk contracts are the direct \texttt{Pi\_wave\_backward} runtime limits,
experimental native CUDA fallback policy, DTS parameter shape precedence when
\texttt{G == S}, genewise-only \texttt{full\_nll\_per\_family()}, lazy resident
prefetch reconfiguration, unowned scheduler/C++ diagnostic exports, and the
legacy C++ leaf-to-species prefix fallback.  These are now documented as
contract gaps before any logic changes.

The tests documentation and integration skip cleanup are also complete.
\texttt{tests/README.md} now states when private-helper guardrail tests are
acceptable, what smoke tests should prove, and how Rust/cargo checks fit into
the CPU-safe test policy.  The HOGENOM unrooted-Newick integration test no
longer inherits an unrelated \texttt{test\_trees\_1000} module-level skip.
\texttt{py\_compile} passed for the edited Python test modules, and
\texttt{pytest --collect-only} reports \texttt{4} tests collected for
\texttt{tests/integration/test\_uniform\_chunked\_model.py}.

The broad verification gate for this continuation reports
\texttt{841 tests collected} and
\texttt{812 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected} for
\texttt{CUDA\_VISIBLE\_DEVICES='' python -m pytest tests/unit -q -m "unit and not gpu"}.

Notebook and script ownership documentation is now explicit.  A new
\texttt{notebooks/README.md} marks both tracked notebooks as checkout-local
HOGENOM analysis artifacts, not portable examples, and the docs index plus main
README point readers there.  \texttt{scripts/README.md} now includes an
ownership matrix for every tracked script, with keep/migrate/delete guidance for
legacy HOGENOM launchers, one-off report builders, validation helpers,
profiling utilities, plotting, and release metadata checks.  Repository hygiene
guards now verify that tracked notebooks are listed in the notebook ownership
note and tracked scripts appear in the ownership matrix.  The focused hygiene
selector reports \texttt{3 passed, 25 deselected}, and
\texttt{py\_compile} passes for the edited hygiene test module.  The full
repository hygiene module now reports \texttt{28 passed}, full collection
reports \texttt{843 tests collected}, and the CPU-only unit gate reports
\texttt{814 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The optimizer-mode documentation gap is now partially closed in the main
README.  The production workflow section lists \texttt{adam},
\texttt{adagrad}, \texttt{lbfgs}, and \texttt{adam-lbfgs}, including the LBFGS
line-search options, LBFGS runtime-failure status, Adam warmup switch, and

that those public modes stay documented.  The Rust backtracking CLI parsing gap
is also fixed: \texttt{--output-dir DIR input.json output.xml} is rejected in
single-sample directory mode instead of silently ignoring the output file.  The
focused Rust parser regression passes, and the focused README guard reports
\texttt{3 passed, 26 deselected}.  Full crate verification reports
\texttt{10} library tests and \texttt{5} CLI tests passing, and the full
repository hygiene module reports \texttt{29 passed}.  Full collection reports
\texttt{844 tests collected}, and the CPU-only unit gate reports
\texttt{815 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The workflow audit's deletion candidate
\texttt{gpurec.workflow.diagnostics.safe\_float()} has been rechecked against

After removal, \texttt{rg} finds \texttt{safe\_float} only in audit

The public workflow optimizer modes now have behavior guards, not just README

\texttt{adam-lbfgs} warmup-to-LBFGS schedule, and the LBFGS runtime-error path

Completed-checkpoint resume behavior is now documented before any status
redesign.  The README states that a resume whose checkpoint \texttt{next\_step}
already equals configured \texttt{steps} performs only final
evaluation/artifact refresh and returns the existing
\texttt{not\_converged}/\texttt{max\_steps} status.  A workflow regression pins
that no optimizer-step row is emitted in this no-op resume path, and a
repository hygiene guard keeps the public README contract present.  The focused
completed-resume selector reports \texttt{2 passed}; the full workflow module
reports \texttt{430 passed}; and the full repository hygiene module reports
\texttt{30 passed}.  Full collection now reports \texttt{849 tests collected},
and the CPU-only unit gate reports
\texttt{820 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

E-adjoint solver nonconvergence now has an explicit workflow contract and
visible telemetry.  The README documents it as diagnostic-only unless the
objective or gradient becomes nonfinite.  \texttt{solver\_stats()} now carries
aggregate E-adjoint iteration, relative-residual, success-batch, and
failed-batch fields into workflow history rows.  The implicit-gradient
docstrings now state that BiCGSTAB returns the current best iterate with
\texttt{\_SolveStats(success=False)} on nonconvergence, and that the retained
E-adjoint gradient path consumes that iterate while forwarding the failure flag
as workflow telemetry.  A finite fake workflow run now records
\texttt{solver/e\_adjoint\_failed\_batches} while still returning
\texttt{not\_converged}, proving the current nonfatal contract at the history
and checkpoint boundary.  The direct \texttt{UniformChunkedReconModel}
\texttt{loss\_and\_grad()} stats payload now exposes
\texttt{e\_adjoint\_method}, \texttt{e\_adjoint\_iterations},
\texttt{e\_adjoint\_rel\_res}, and \texttt{e\_adjoint\_success} from the same
retained E-adjoint solve, without changing loss, gradient, scaling, or workflow
behavior.  The focused guard originally failed before this
diagnostics change because \texttt{solver/e\_adjoint\_iterations\_max} was
missing, then passed after the aggregation change.  Current verification
compiles the touched implicit-gradient, telemetry, workflow, and hygiene
modules; the focused BiCGSTAB/E-adjoint selector reports \texttt{6 passed};
the full repository hygiene module reports \texttt{46 passed}; and full
collection reports \texttt{883 tests collected}.

Direct uniform-chunk E-adjoint telemetry is now exposed for sampled-chunk and
stochastic direct optimizers.  \texttt{\_evaluate\_chunked\_uniform()} maps the
retained solve stats into \texttt{e\_adjoint\_method},
\texttt{e\_adjoint\_iterations}, \texttt{e\_adjoint\_rel\_res}, and
\texttt{e\_adjoint\_success}; \texttt{loss\_and\_grad()} preserves those keys
while adding reduction metadata.  README and direct API docstrings now define
the public stats payload before further telemetry changes.  The focused direct
telemetry selector reports \texttt{3 passed}; the workflow telemetry selector
still reports \texttt{2 passed}; the full optimization workflow module reports
\texttt{30 passed}; repository hygiene reports \texttt{49 passed}; and full
collection reports \texttt{888 tests collected}.

The native CUDA prototype launchers now share the documented dynamic
shared-memory preflight contract.  Both the self-loop and Pibar launchers
compute \texttt{shared\_bytes}, check it against the device opt-in shared-memory
limit, set \texttt{CU\_FUNC\_ATTRIBUTE\_MAX\_DYNAMIC\_SHARED\_SIZE\_BYTES}, and
pass the same byte count to \texttt{cuLaunchKernel}.  The self-loop path now
raises a clear \texttt{ValueError} for oversized scratch before reaching the
driver attribute/launch call, matching the Pibar prototype's preflight shape.
A repository hygiene guard pins this source-level launch contract until a CUDA
smoke can exercise required-mode prototype paths directly.  \texttt{py\_compile}
passes for the touched CUDA prototype and hygiene modules; the focused launch
contract selector reports \texttt{3 passed}; repository hygiene reports
\texttt{50 passed}; and full collection reports \texttt{889 tests collected}.

Historical benchmark provenance is now separated from clean-checkout

A repository hygiene guard now scans README/docs inline path references under

The branchscale penalty report script now documents its legacy input contract
before any loader rewrite.  \texttt{scripts/make\_hogenom\_branchscale\_penalty\_report.py}

The checkout-local configuration docs now make the CUDA boundary explicit

The Rust backtracking schema finding is now reflected as completed in the
current audit state instead of remaining stale in the initial findings list.
\texttt{crates/gpurec-backtrack/src/lib.rs} already documents the JSON payload
schema: row-major matrix layout, base-2 log units, the \texttt{-1e300}
negative-infinity sentinel, postorder species indexing, clade-indexed

The unsupported checkpoint-config helper surface is now documented as removed.
\texttt{README.md} tells Python tooling to read
\texttt{load\_checkpoint(path)["config"]} from
\texttt{gpurec.workflow.checkpoint} and reconstruct with
\texttt{RunConfig.from\_dict(...)}, with no separate public
\texttt{load\_checkpoint\_config} helper.  The audit finding now reflects that
the helper is absent from package code rather than describing it as a current
definition-only deletion candidate.  Workflow export coverage explicitly keeps
\texttt{load\_checkpoint\_config} out of \texttt{gpurec.workflow.\_\_all\_\_}
and top-level \texttt{gpurec.\_\_all\_\_}.  The touched hygiene/workflow tests
compile, the focused checkpoint/Rust selector reports \texttt{3 passed},
repository hygiene reports \texttt{55 passed}, and full collection reports
\texttt{895 tests collected}.

The public full-batch helper contracts are now documented beside their existing
CPU-safe behavior guards.  \texttt{README.md} and
\texttt{GeneReconModel.materialize\_batches()} state that materialization builds
every resident batch static state and returns a copy of the batch metadata list.
\texttt{README.md} and \texttt{GeneReconModel.full\_loss\_for\_theta(theta)}
state that differentiable explicit-theta probes use the gradient-producing
streaming path, while \texttt{torch.no\_grad()} calls use the loss-only path.
The audit finding now records this as covered instead of only indirectly

The small-species backward limitation is now documented as retained fast-path
behavior before any fallback implementation work.  The README and docs map
state that the checked minimal CUDA config is a source-tree config/parser
fixture, not a CPU fallback and not an end-to-end optimizer smoke, because the
retained Pi backward/gradient path currently requires more than 256 postorder
species nodes.  \texttt{gpurec/core/backward.py} documents the same CUDA,
\texttt{float32}/\texttt{float64}, and \texttt{S > 256} contract at the source
boundary.  Focused repository and release guards pin the wording so future
small-\texttt{S} runtime work starts from an explicit public limitation.

Direct DTS parameter shape precedence is now documented before any runtime
unification.  Public \texttt{GeneReconModel} paths use unambiguous
\texttt{global} \texttt{[3]}, \texttt{specieswise} \texttt{[S, 3]}, and
\texttt{genewise} \texttt{[G, 3]} theta shapes, with genewise scalar event
vectors normalized to \texttt{[G, 1]} before retained DTS kernels.  README and
source docstrings now tell direct callers to avoid bare \texttt{[G]} DTS
vectors when \texttt{G == S}: the direct forward helper treats a
one-dimensional length-\texttt{S} tensor as shared species-indexed, while the
retained backward helper with \texttt{family\_idx} treats a one-dimensional
tensor as family-indexed.  The new hygiene guard pins README plus
\texttt{Pi\_wave\_forward()}, \texttt{\_prepare\_param()}, and
\texttt{\_dts\_layout\_param\_args()} docstrings; \texttt{py\_compile} passes
for the touched source and hygiene modules, and the focused DTS documentation
selector reports \texttt{1 passed}.  The full repository hygiene module reports
\texttt{58 passed}, and full collection reports \texttt{898 tests collected}.

The workflow \texttt{log\_every} option is now documented according to the
actual runner behavior.  The optimizer records history JSONL every step through
\texttt{self.\_record(row)}; \texttt{config.log\_every} only gates the stdout
progress print.  README, CLI help, and the \texttt{RunConfig} source comment
now describe it as console progress throttling rather than history logging.
The touched CLI/config/hygiene modules compile, and the focused documentation
guard reports \texttt{1 passed}.  The full repository hygiene module reports
\texttt{59 passed}, and full collection reports \texttt{899 tests collected}.

A follow-up read-only subagent refresh added the next documented deletion and

table before deletion.

The workflow checkpoint submodule support boundary is now explicit before any
helper rename or export cleanup.  README and
\texttt{gpurec/workflow/checkpoint.py} state that the stable shortcut surface is
\texttt{gpurec.workflow}/top-level \texttt{gpurec}, while
\texttt{gpurec.workflow.checkpoint} supports
\texttt{CHECKPOINT\_VERSION}, \texttt{save\_checkpoint},
\texttt{load\_checkpoint}, \texttt{restore\_model\_theta}, and
\texttt{validate\_checkpoint\_model\_compatibility} for advanced tooling that
needs the versioned checkpoint payload.  The hygiene guard keeps those helpers
out of the lazy top-level exports unless they are intentionally promoted.  The
touched checkpoint/hygiene modules compile, and the focused support-boundary
guard reports \texttt{1 passed}.

The v1 checkpoint identity boundary is now documented according to the current
runtime before any stricter resume-validation change.  README and
\texttt{gpurec/workflow/checkpoint.py} state that workflow checkpoints carry
\texttt{family\_names}, \texttt{species\_names}, and config identity fields
\texttt{species\_tree}, \texttt{families\_file}, \texttt{mode},
\texttt{start}, and \texttt{max\_families}.  \texttt{load\_checkpoint()} requires
those fields to exist and validates name-list metadata, while
\texttt{validate\_checkpoint\_model\_compatibility()} compares them against the
active \texttt{RunConfig} and rebuilt model before
\texttt{restore\_model\_theta()} copies parameters, normalizing only path
identity fields.  The loader still does not reconstruct a full
\texttt{RunConfig}; callers that need complete config type and range validation
should pass \texttt{payload["config"]} through
\texttt{RunConfig.from\_dict(...)}.  The touched checkpoint and hygiene modules
compile, the focused checkpoint identity documentation guard reports
\texttt{1 passed}, the full repository hygiene module reports \texttt{63
passed}, and full collection reports \texttt{903 tests collected}.

The scheduler and C++ extension pruning plan now has the ownership table needed

classifies \texttt{preprocess\_multiple\_families}, the runtime scheduler/layout

The documented uniform forward/backward profiling benchmark is importable again

The penalty-316 branchscale KKT script now has a source/help/ownership contract

The active-mask bf16 boundary is now documented without changing dtype

path still supports only fp32/fp64 and rejects bf16 before the helper is

The genewise-only per-family NLL contract is now explicit before any broader
shared-theta implementation work.  The README and
\texttt{GeneReconModel.full\_nll\_per\_family()} docstring state that

genewise-only public surfaces for independent per-family losses, while global
and specieswise modes should use \texttt{forward(reduce="per\_family")} under

CPU-safe guards verify genewise delegation with \texttt{need\_grad=False},
shared-mode rejection, and README coverage.  The focused workflow selector
reports \texttt{3 passed, 432 deselected}; the README guard reports

collection reports \texttt{855 tests collected}; and the CPU-only unit gate
reports \texttt{826 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The stale \texttt{GPUREC\_LEAF\_HIT\_ONLY\_LOGP} diagnostic switch is now
removed from the retained wave-backward kernel wrapper and the public README
environment table.  The removal was documented first because source inspection
showed the flag was not a supported environment contract: it was read by Python
and passed as a Triton constexpr, but the constexpr was not read inside the

A Rust subagent rechecked \texttt{crates/gpurec-backtrack} for the next
deletion candidates.  It found no direct \texttt{quick-xml} usage in
\texttt{src/main.rs} or \texttt{src/lib.rs}; \texttt{quick-xml} is already
available transitively through \texttt{rustree}, so the direct dev-dependency is

verification reports \texttt{cargo check --all-targets} passing and

Repository hygiene guards now prevent reintroducing the direct
\texttt{quick-xml} dependency or optional work-item clade state.  The focused
Python hygiene selector reports \texttt{2 passed}, the full repository hygiene
module reports \texttt{35 passed}, full collection reports
\texttt{858 tests collected}, and the CPU-only unit gate reports
\texttt{829 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The lazy-prefetch solver reconfiguration contract is now public before any
runtime behavior change.  \texttt{README.md} and
\texttt{GeneReconModel.configure\_solver\_iterations()} state that
reconfiguration updates model defaults and resident batch static states that are
already built, but does not cancel or rewrite pending background prefetch work.
Users should configure before scheduling lazy prefetch, or materialize resident
batches and configure again when all batches should share new controls.  The
focused repository hygiene guard reports \texttt{1 passed}, and
\texttt{py\_compile} passes for the touched Python modules.  The full
repository hygiene module now reports \texttt{39 passed}, full collection
reports \texttt{862 tests collected}, and the CPU-only unit gate reports
\texttt{833 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

A fresh set of five read-only subagents re-audited the current tracked
inventory across core Python runtime, public API/workflow/optimization, native
kernels/C++/Rust, tests/CI, and docs/scripts/notebooks/configuration.  The
consolidated findings are recorded in
\texttt{docs/repo-wide-audit-2026-05-21.md}.  The most actionable new open
items are a bf16 support-policy mismatch between direct APIs and workflow docs;
implicit-gradient solver-failure policy; inconsistent native CUDA loader and
shared-memory preflight behavior; remaining scheduler/helper diagnostic
ownership decisions; RecPhyloXML
multi-tree assumptions; test fixture documentation and simplification; and the
ongoing release license blocker.  Documentation-first targets from that pass
are bf16 support policy, kernel/prototype environment policy, scheduler
diagnostic ownership, fixture contracts, and legacy script/notebook retention
decisions.

The supported simple-Newick input subset is now documented before any parser
behavior changes.  \texttt{README.md}, \texttt{GeneReconModel} construction
docstrings, \texttt{UniformChunkedReconModel} docstrings, \texttt{GeneDataset},
and the preprocessing pybind docstrings now state that the retained parser
accepts unquoted labels, optional internal labels, numeric branch lengths that
are ignored, one rooted binary species tree, and semicolon-delimited multi-tree
gene files that are amalgamated per family.  They also record the unsupported
surface: quoted or escaped labels, comments, NHX/BEAST-style metadata, embedded
delimiter characters, unary gene nodes, and non-binary species nodes.  A
repository hygiene guard now keeps that public contract visible.  Verification
reports \texttt{py\_compile} passing for the touched Python modules, the
focused Newick documentation guard reporting \texttt{1 passed}, the full
repository hygiene module reporting \texttt{41 passed}, and full collection
reporting \texttt{865 tests collected}.

The Newick parser contract is now also pinned with CPU preprocessing
regressions.  The acceptance case covers unquoted labels, internal labels,
ignored numeric branch lengths, optional terminal semicolon on a final record,
and deterministic right-binarization of a multifurcating gene tree.  Rejection
cases cover multiple species trees in one species file, non-binary species
trees, unary gene nodes, and unsupported branch-length metadata/comment syntax.
The first focused run failed because the public documentation overclaimed
whitespace support after a branch-length colon; the README and fixture now state
the actual parser requirement that the numeric branch length immediately follow
\texttt{:}.  The focused parser selector reports \texttt{5 passed}, the full
AleRax/preprocessing input module reports \texttt{34 passed}, repository
hygiene remains \texttt{41 passed}, and full collection reports
\texttt{870 tests collected}.  The CPU-only unit gate reports
\texttt{841 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

Fixture contracts for the smallest stochastic backtracking inputs are now
checked in and guarded.  \texttt{tests/data/test\_trees\_3/README.md}
documents the CUDA-only single-family fixture, including the expected
\texttt{35 x 15} exported \texttt{pi} shape, one \texttt{recGeneTree}, and
10-leaf sampling smoke.  The stochastic backtracking integration test now uses
named constants for those expectations instead of bare magic values.
\texttt{tests/fixtures/backtracking/README.md} documents the hand-authored
CPU-only Rust JSON fixture: three postorder species, three clades, one
deterministic root split, row-major \texttt{3 x 3} base-2 log matrix, and root
origination mass.  The Rust fixture integration test now asserts that JSON
shape before invoking the CLI.  A repository hygiene guard keeps both fixture
contracts linked from \texttt{tests/README.md} and tied to the checked JSON and
test constants.  Verification reports \texttt{py\_compile} passing for the
touched tests, the focused fixture-contract guard reporting \texttt{1 passed},
the Rust JSON integration fixture reporting \texttt{2 passed}, stochastic
backtracking collection reporting \texttt{2 collected}, repository hygiene
reporting \texttt{42 passed}, full collection reporting
\texttt{872 tests collected}, and the CPU-only unit gate reporting
\texttt{842 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The \texttt{leaf\_col\_index} upper-bound validation gap from that refresh is
now closed.  Family preprocessing validation accepts only species indexes in
\texttt{[0, S)}, so malformed entries cannot become out-of-range
\texttt{leaf\_species\_index} values in the forward layout.  A focused
regression tampers a family to contain species index \texttt{S}, confirms
the cache path rejects it without rerunning preprocessing, and reports
\texttt{1 passed}.  The neighboring AleRax/cache unit module reports
\texttt{29 passed}; \texttt{py\_compile} passes for the touched Python modules.
Full collection reports \texttt{863 tests collected}, and the CPU-only unit
gate reports \texttt{834 passed, 1 skipped, 6 deselected}.

The Rust sampler payload validation gaps from the refreshed audit are now
closed.  \texttt{PreparedBacktracker::new()} rejects non-finite base-2 log
values in matrix and vector payloads, non-finite split log probabilities,
non-finite or negative origination weights, and \texttt{leaf\_species} indexes
outside the species dimension before derived sampler weights are computed.  The
focused Rust selector first failed the new non-finite and out-of-range
\texttt{leaf\_species} regressions, then reported \texttt{6 passed} after the
validation change.  \texttt{cargo fmt}, \texttt{cargo check --all-targets}, and
\texttt{cargo test} pass; the full Rust test run reports \texttt{12} library
tests, \texttt{5} CLI tests, and \texttt{0} doc tests passing.

The test subprocess timeout cleanup is now complete.  Every
\texttt{subprocess.run()} call under \texttt{tests/} has an explicit timeout,
with a longer timeout for Cargo-based Rust integration smoke tests and shorter
timeouts for Python and Git subprocesses.  A repository hygiene guard scans
tracked tests with \texttt{ast} and rejects subprocess calls without a
\texttt{timeout=} keyword.  The focused guard reports \texttt{1 passed}; the
edited examples/global-scheduler/CLI slice reports \texttt{6 passed}; release
metadata reports \texttt{39 passed, 1 skipped}; the workflow import/export
selector reports \texttt{14 passed, 420 deselected}; and the Rust fixture
integration smoke reports \texttt{1 passed}.

The release/CI workflow assertions are now structured around the checked YAML
contract rather than whole-file text mirrors.  \texttt{tests/unit/test\_release\_metadata.py}
parses \texttt{.github/workflows/cpu-unit.yml} with PyYAML and asserts the

The strict diagnostics JSON helper now documents its sanitizing behavior at the
definition site.  \texttt{json\_dumps\_strict()} writes standards-compliant JSON
after converting non-finite floats to JSON \texttt{null}; the name is therefore
about the emitted JSON grammar, not about rejecting diagnostics that contain
\texttt{NaN} or \texttt{Infinity}.  A repository hygiene guard pins that
docstring so future workflow callers do not mistake the helper for a rejecting
serializer.  \texttt{py\_compile} passes for the edited diagnostics and hygiene
modules, and the focused diagnostics selector reports \texttt{3 passed}.

The bf16 dtype policy is now explicit before any kernel or optimizer behavior
change.  The README and \texttt{UniformChunkedReconModel} docstring state that
\texttt{torch.bfloat16} is an experimental direct-API-only dtype for CUDA
forward/NLL probes, while workflow configuration and CLI runs intentionally
support only \texttt{float32} and \texttt{float64}.  The docs also call out that
the retained Pi backward/gradient path does not support bf16, so bf16 should
not be used for release smokes, optimizer checkpoints, or Hessian/second-order
diagnostics.  A repository hygiene guard keeps README, direct API docs, CLI,
and workflow config aligned, and a workflow unit regression checks that
\texttt{dtype\_from\_name("bf16")} and \texttt{GeneReconModel} reject bf16
while \texttt{UniformChunkedReconModel}'s dtype gate accepts it.  The focused
bf16 selector reports \texttt{4 passed}, and \texttt{py\_compile} passes for
the touched API and test modules.  The broader repository hygiene suite reports
\texttt{44 passed}, the workflow dtype selector reports
\texttt{27 passed, 410 deselected}, and release metadata remains
\texttt{39 passed, 1 skipped}.  Full collection reports
\texttt{877 tests collected}.

The prepared-origination-probability fast path is now documented as an internal
trust boundary.  \texttt{prepare\_origination\_probs()} explains that
\texttt{assume\_prepared=True} is only for model/static-owned tensors that
already passed constructor preparation: shape compatibility is still checked
after device/dtype conversion, but finite, nonnegative, positive-mass, and
normalization checks are skipped.  A focused regression verifies that default
preparation rejects zero-mass, negative, and \texttt{NaN} inputs while the
prepared path returns those tensors unchanged, and a repository hygiene guard
keeps the trust-boundary docstring in place.  \texttt{py\_compile} passes for
the touched likelihood and test modules, and the focused selector reports
\texttt{4 passed}.  The full origination-probability module reports
\texttt{9 passed}, repository hygiene reports \texttt{45 passed}, and full
collection reports \texttt{881 tests collected}.

The native CUDA prototype loader/fallback policy is now documented before any
runtime consolidation.  The README states that CUDA prototype flags are
experimental diagnostics: \texttt{auto} and \texttt{enabled} are best effort,
required tokens re-raise failures only after dtype/device/layout eligibility
selects a prototype, and \texttt{GPUREC\_CUDA\_PIBAR\_FROM\_UD\_STRICT=1}
makes the selected Pibar path strict even from \texttt{auto}.  Source docstrings
now pin the current asymmetry: self-loop fallback is silent and catches only
\texttt{ImportError}, \texttt{RuntimeError}, and \texttt{ValueError};
Pibar fallback is silent in \texttt{auto}, warns once in \texttt{enabled}, and
the self-loop loader preloads wheel NVRTC builtins while the Pibar loader
currently does not.  Consolidating loader preflight, exception breadth, or
warning behavior remains a documented future runtime change.  Verification
compiles the touched helper, backward, kernel-loader, and test modules; the
focused mode/fallback selectors report \texttt{22 passed, 21 deselected} and
\texttt{2 passed}; the full core helper/backward slice reports
\texttt{45 passed}; repository hygiene reports \texttt{48 passed}; and full
collection reports \texttt{886 tests collected}.

The Python 3.10 TOML fallback finding is now covered without changing release

The tests/Rust/docs explorer found a second Python 3.10 TOML issue before the

The Python 3.10 collection issue is now fixed: the TOML-reading unit modules use

The workflow/scripts explorer added two more decision-table rows before any

The core/API explorer read the current core, kernel, C++, API, and related test

The Rust CLI multi-sample output gap is now guarded.  The CPU-only fixture

The direct \texttt{build\_wave\_layout()} family metadata gap is now guarded and

The final optimization evaluation nonfinite gap is now guarded and fixed.  The

The broader affected-module verification remains green after the final-eval

The resume optimizer-state exception boundary is now guarded and fixed.  The

\subsection{Local HOGENOM Script Model Cleanup}

The local profiling and AleRax comparison script model-lifetime gap is now

AleRax comparison helper samples through

locally constructed \texttt{GeneReconModel} after successful work and also close
it if likelihood evaluation, sampling, or event-count extraction raises.

The fake-model legacy-script regressions cover successful cleanup and exception
cleanup for both scripts.  The touched profiling script, comparison script, and
legacy-script test module compile, and the cleanup-only legacy-script unit
module reported \texttt{25 passed}.  The full repository hygiene module reports
\texttt{71 passed} after the audit-row update.

\subsection{Local HOGENOM Script Count Validation}

The local profiling and AleRax comparison scripts now reject invalid count
controls at the parser boundary.  Both checkout-local CLIs use shared
\texttt{gpurec/\_argparse\_types.py} helpers for positive, non-negative, and
positive-even values, so chunk size, family count, sample count, start index,
seed, solver iterations, and wave size fail with direct argparse diagnostics
instead of reaching file loading, CUDA checks, or lower-level model validation.

The legacy-script parser regression first failed because the scripts had no
testable parser builders.  Both scripts now expose \texttt{build\_parser()},
and the focused invalid-control selector reports \texttt{12 passed}.  The full
legacy-script unit module now reports \texttt{37 passed}, and the full
repository hygiene module still reports \texttt{71 passed}.

\subsection{Explicit Theta Shape Validation}

The explicit-theta decision is now documented before changing behavior.
Checkout-local raw theta tensors should match the active parameter-sharing mode
exactly: \texttt{[3]} for global models, \texttt{[S, 3]} for specieswise
models, and \texttt{[G, 3]} for genewise models.  Short tensors, extra event
columns, wrong ranks, and wrong mode row counts should fail before CUDA checks,
theta cloning, or full-batch streaming parameter extraction.

That validation cleanup is now complete.  Shared
\texttt{validate\_theta\_shape()} enforces the exact raw tensor shape in the
\texttt{GeneReconModel} constructor for \texttt{theta\_init} and in
\texttt{full\_loss\_for\_theta()} for explicit \texttt{theta} tensors.  The
focused invalid-shape selector first reproduced \texttt{12} failures, then
reports \texttt{12 passed}; the full validation unit module reports
\texttt{50 passed}; and the existing \texttt{full\_loss\_for\_theta()} streaming
contract guard reports \texttt{1 passed} after updating its fixture to use a
valid global theta shape.
The full repository hygiene module reports \texttt{74 passed}, and the full
workflow unit module reports \texttt{444 passed} after validating explicit theta
shapes on the public \texttt{full\_loss\_for\_theta()} path.

\subsection{Parameter Extraction Shape Contract}

The \texttt{gpurec/core/extract\_parameters.py} contract decision is now
documented before changing tests or docstrings.  The current shape policy is
retained: \texttt{family\_rows} disambiguates bare one-dimensional tensors, so
when \texttt{G == S} a length-\texttt{G} vector is treated as family rows before
the length-\texttt{S} species-row interpretation.  The follow-up guard should
cover global, specieswise, genewise, \texttt{family\_rows}, and the
\texttt{G == S} ambiguity before any refactor changes this helper family.

That guard is now in place.  The direct CPU table module covers
\texttt{as\_family\_param()}, \texttt{as\_family\_species()}, and
\texttt{extract\_parameters\_uniform()} for global, specieswise, genewise,
\texttt{family\_rows}, and \texttt{G == S} semantics.  The first focused run
exposed the missing \texttt{as\_family\_species()} contract docstring; after
adding it and correcting the independent base-2 log-softmax oracle, the module
reports \texttt{8 passed}.
The focused extraction/pruning-plan selector reports \texttt{9 passed}, and the
full repository hygiene module reports \texttt{74 passed} after updating the
source guards for this coverage.

\subsection{Test-Only Scheduler Helper Deletion}

The scheduler-helper deletion decision is now documented before changing
runtime source.  \texttt{collate\_wave()} and \texttt{split\_phase\_waves()}
have no tracked production callers, are not exposed by the high-level public
exports, and are only exercised by helper-level unit tests.  The cleanup should
remove those helpers from \texttt{gpurec.core.batching}, remove the direct
helper tests, and add a source hygiene guard so tracked runtime Python does not
grow new references to either name.

That deletion is now complete.  \texttt{collate\_wave()} and

The broader scheduler/hygiene selector reports \texttt{138 passed} across

\subsection{Python Clade-Wave Adapter Deletion}

The \texttt{compute\_clade\_waves()} Python adapter deletion decision is now
documented before changing runtime source.  The Python helper has no tracked
production caller, is not exposed by high-level public exports, and only adapts
already-preprocessed C++ \texttt{phased\_waves} /
\texttt{phased\_phases} metadata for helper-level tests.  This does not remove
the C++ implementation with the same name; preprocessing still owns phased-wave
generation.  The cleanup should delete \texttt{gpurec/core/scheduling.py}, drop
the helper-level unit module, and add a source guard so the Python adapter does
not return to tracked runtime source.

That cleanup is now complete.  \texttt{gpurec/core/scheduling.py} and
\texttt{tests/unit/test\_scheduling.py} were deleted, and
\texttt{tests/README.md} no longer lists the removed unit module.  The retained
C++ helper with the same name remains in \texttt{preprocess.cpp}; repository
hygiene now guards that the Python adapter module and helper name stay out of
tracked runtime Python source.
The focused scheduler/family-layout/hygiene selector reports \texttt{58 passed}
after updating the ownership table and source guard for the Python clade-wave
adapter deletion.
The broader scheduler/family-layout/hygiene selector reports \texttt{131 passed}
after removing the adapter and its helper-level unit module.
The consolidated touched-unit baseline now reports \texttt{664 passed} across

family-layout, and repository-hygiene unit modules.

\subsection{Top-Level Low-Level Import Documentation}

The public package documentation cleanup is now documented before changing the

imports from \texttt{gpurec.core.model}, \texttt{gpurec.core.likelihood}, and

classifies \texttt{gpurec.core} as unstable implementation surface except for

\texttt{gpurec}, \texttt{gpurec.api}, and \texttt{gpurec.workflow} entry points

That cleanup is now complete.  The package docstring now describes the
high-level public API, shows \texttt{GeneReconModel} plus workflow imports, and

the package docstring does not advertise the old core import examples.  The
touched modules compile, and the focused package-doc guard reports

\subsection{Likelihood Alias Deprecation}

The likelihood compatibility-alias behavior change is now documented before

\texttt{DeprecationWarning}s from those aliases, move ordinary tests to
\texttt{compute\_nll()} and \texttt{compute\_nll\_root\_rows()}, and keep one
explicit compatibility test for the old names.

That cleanup is now complete.  Both aliases warn with
\texttt{DeprecationWarning}, the CUDA-marked specieswise helper test imports the
current \texttt{compute\_nll*} helpers, and repository hygiene guards that the
legacy names appear only in their implementation and the direct compatibility
test.  The touched modules compile, the origination-probability plus
alias-owner selector reports \texttt{10 passed}, the specieswise module
collection check reports \texttt{5 tests collected}, and the full repository
hygiene module reports \texttt{78 passed}.

\subsection{Fresh Subagent Findings And Profiling Ownership}

Two read-only subagent refresh passes returned new ranked findings for the
current worktree.  The native/C++/kernel pass ranked production-auto native
CUDA prototype routing and unowned direct pybind scheduler/stat exports as the
highest risks, followed by the legacy \texttt{preprocess} pybind, DTS direct
kernel shape ambiguity, thin direct characterization for retained backward
kernel wrappers, and broad env-driven launch tuning.  The workflow/scripts pass
ranked the legacy HOGENOM optimizer launchers as the largest untested script
surface, followed by duplicated validation, weaker legacy cleanup windows,
unclear profiling ownership, source-checkout-only configs that can be mistaken
for installed workflows, and historical branchscale/KKT scripts that need
migration or archival.

The DTS direct-kernel one-dimensional parameter ambiguity finding is now closed
for retained production code by the shared
\texttt{\_dts\_layout\_contract.py} classifier and
\texttt{tests/unit/test\_dts\_layout\_contract.py}.  Direct callers still must
use \texttt{[G, 1]} or \texttt{[G, S]} when \texttt{G == S}; the contract
documents and tests the current forward/backward precedence before any behavior
change.

The retained backward direct-wrapper characterization slice is now guarded by
CPU-safe tests for pre-launch metadata validation in the uniform backward
wrapper, DTS Pibar staging wrapper, and DTS-staged Pibar VJP wrapper.  These
tests pin the direct caller contract without requiring CUDA or reaching Triton
launch.

The broad env-driven kernel launch tuning finding is now closed for package
runtime.  Tracked kernel modules carry no \texttt{GPUREC\_*} literals or direct
environment reads, package code reads only the supported environment owner
manifest, and repository hygiene guards both contracts.

The family path/name/map normalization slice of the duplicated-validation
finding is now addressed for dataset construction and the uniform chunked API
through shared \texttt{gpurec.core.model.normalize\_family\_inputs()} handling.

The integer-control slice of the duplicated-validation finding is now reduced:
\texttt{gpurec.\_validation} owns \texttt{integer\_value()},
\texttt{positive\_int()}, \texttt{nonnegative\_int()}, and
\texttt{positive\_even\_int()}.  The API validation module re-exports those
support helpers, and \texttt{RunConfig} string adapters delegate to them for
workflow integer controls.  Optional integer controls now follow that same
support boundary through \texttt{optional\_positive\_int()},
\texttt{optional\_nonnegative\_int()}, and
\texttt{optional\_positive\_even\_int()}, including workflow string adapters,
core AleRax family-selection validation, and shared batch-planning controls
such as \texttt{clade\_budget} and \texttt{family\_chunk\_size}.  The
resident uniform memory-policy estimators now delegate integer dimensions and
candidate controls to the shared helpers too.  The Rust scheduler bridge keeps
only its string adapter locally and delegates non-string integer semantics to
the same shared validation helper.  Checkpoint resume metadata now keeps its
checkpoint-specific error wording while using the shared nonnegative-integer
validator.  The resident-model \texttt{prefetch\_batches} adapter keeps only
its \texttt{all}/disabled string aliases locally before delegating count
semantics to that shared helper.  The stochastic backtracking bridge now keeps
seed/event range checks locally while delegating integral-number coercion to
the shared integer helper.

The float-range slice now follows the same ownership boundary:
\texttt{gpurec.\_validation} owns \texttt{finite\_float()},
\texttt{positive\_float()}, and \texttt{nonnegative\_float()}.  The API
validation module re-exports those helpers for direct API callers, while
workflow float adapters already delegate to the shared validator.
Checkpoint resume metadata now delegates finite-float semantics to that helper
too.  The Pi-adjoint fixed-point relaxation control now delegates through a
core solver helper to the shared positive-float validator while preserving the
retained backward path's fixed legacy error wording.  Torch-specific
bool-tensor detection remains lazy so metadata imports stay lightweight.

The profiling ownership boundary documentation gap from that pass is now
addressed.  \texttt{profiling/README.md} records the two tracked profiling
entrypoints, their source-checkout/CUDA/local-data assumptions, output-contract
expectations, ignored artifact policy, and bytecode-only
\texttt{profiling/proposal2/} / \texttt{profiling/proposal8/} scratch
directories.  The main README and docs map now point to this note, and
repository hygiene guards that every tracked profiling Python file is listed in
the profiling README and that ignored artifact roots stay documented.

The public-API/docs refresh pass then flagged release metadata license blockers
and the Rust sampling binary distribution model.  The license decision remains
the only open release blocker from that pair.  The binary model is now captured
by the release-readiness contract as an external-binary wheel expectation plus a
locked source-archive Cargo fallback.  The pass also identified an
inconsistency between the new \texttt{gpurec.core} internal-stability wording
and the README's
\texttt{GeneDataset(..., leaf\_species\_maps=...)} guidance, broad
\texttt{core.batch\_planning} exports, retained deprecated likelihood aliases,
private API helper modules without clear support notes, duplicated evaluator
and gradient paths, scheduler complexity, public helper/property documentation
gaps, stale ignored notebook artifacts, and hygiene tests that deliberately
preserve some private or deprecated surfaces.

The \texttt{GeneDataset(..., leaf\_species\_maps=...)} inconsistency is now
fixed as documentation only.  The README and docs map describe it as a narrow
low-level preprocessing/mapping exception for custom gene-leaf to species maps,
while the rest of \texttt{gpurec.core} remains unstable unless explicitly
documented.  \texttt{GeneReconModel.from\_trees()} also points users to this
narrow exception for labels that cannot use prefix fallback or AleRax
\texttt{mapping} entries.  The touched modules compile, and the focused package
docs, mapping-contract, profiling README, ignored-workspace inventory, and
likelihood-alias owner selector reports \texttt{5 passed}.

The broad \texttt{gpurec.core.batch\_planning.\_\_all\_\_} finding is also
resolved as documentation and guard tightening.  Because API, workflow, CLI,

\texttt{normalize\_batch\_packing()}, \texttt{normalize\_clade\_budget()},
\texttt{normalize\_family\_chunk\_size()}, and
\texttt{plan\_family\_batches()}, narrowing the export set would be a behavior
change.  The runtime-surface plan and module docstring now classify the exact

The touched modules compile, and the focused batch-planning export/runtime-plan
selector reports \texttt{2 passed}.
The release metadata checker still fails on the expected policy blockers:

The simplification opportunity index is now part of the guarded audit

The private \texttt{gpurec.api} helper-module documentation gap is now

The config ownership documentation gap is now addressed.  A new

Strict artifact-gate failures now use the same wording.  The

Current operator docs now use the same distinction.  The README, run-config
reference, production optimization guide, output artifact contract, and input

\texttt{--require-production-default-route}.

Release-readiness smoke documentation now uses the same gate wording.  The
installed help checks describe mode-default optimizer gates separately from the

The README and output artifact contract now avoid the ambiguous "shipped

CLI route-gate wording now has one source of truth in \texttt{gpurec.cli}.
The \texttt{--require-mode-default-optimizer} help text says mode default

The shared \texttt{--require-production-default-route} help text now spells out

\texttt{effective\_route\_metadata()} now emits the production route contract

\texttt{production\_default\_optimizer\_config\_overrides()} now exposes the

\texttt{gpurec config-template} command and source example checks reuse that
helper, keeping installed starter templates tied to the same genewise
\texttt{hessian-sgd} and specieswise \texttt{adagrad-restarts} route metadata
audits.

Generated \texttt{gpurec config-template --mode genewise} and
\texttt{--mode specieswise} JSON now round-trip through \texttt{RunConfig} in
unit coverage and must report \texttt{uses\_production\_default\_route=true}.
This guards the installed starter configs before any CUDA likelihood model is
built.

Generated templates now also pass the public
\texttt{validate-config --require-mode-default-optimizer
--require-production-default-route} path when pointed at existing AleRax
inputs.  This guards the installed starter workflow end to end through CLI
preflight without constructing the CUDA likelihood model.

The package workflow's installed-wheel smoke now generates genewise and
specieswise templates from the installed \texttt{gpurec config-template}
command, points them at the checked tiny AleRax fixture paths, and validates
both with \texttt{gpurec validate-config --require-mode-default-optimizer
--require-production-default-route}.  Release-readiness documentation and
release metadata tests track the same operator path.

The package workflow's source-archive example smoke now runs both checked
example configs through \texttt{validate-config
--require-mode-default-optimizer --require-production-default-route
--check-preprocess}.  Unpacked source artifacts must therefore prove the shipped
genewise and specieswise route gates while still exercising the retained CPU
preprocessing path.

\section{Next Audit Targets}

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
  \item Choose and add a redistributable license, then update
        \texttt{pyproject.toml} license metadata and license classifier.
  \item Add an installed-artifact sampling smoke if a future release ships a
        bundled backtracking binary; the current contract keeps wheels on an
        external-binary requirement and source archives on the locked Cargo
        fallback.
  \item Re-scan workflow internals for the next low-risk reuse or boundary
        improvement now that artifact publication, metadata validation, and
        summary JSON handling have shared helpers.
  \item Continue auditing public API consistency, documentation coverage, and
        CPU-safe regression tests for user-facing workflows.
  \item Keep committing small, verified increments so the branch remains
        reviewable despite the larger audit scope.
\end{enumerate}

\end{document}
